# AI Media Scout — 2026-11-10

Expanded open-source creative-AI research across 96 fields, 815 repository queries and 23 model tasks, supplemented by live primary sources beyond GitHub. This edition provides 28 complete guides and 161 additional eligible screened discoveries; four guides complete earlier pending entries. It includes local media/voice workflows, agent-driven creative tools, fabrication experiments, photobook layout and learned print watermarking. No discovered application was installed or run. New entries remain Quality unverified or explicitly Experimental where current prototype evidence supports that label. A separate, scoped OIDN library assessment supports Ready to try for CPU single-frame denoising, with results still documented and excluded workflows stated. All 2,215 previously published tools already had current-week reviews; new entries receive today’s six-check assessment. Raw unreviewed, duplicate, non-AI and unresolved-license candidates stay local or in the watchlist rather than becoming library entries. The collector was interrupted after all 815 search queries and 23 model-task responses were captured. Recovery reuses those same-day search captures and completes repository reads; it is not a second fresh search. Original-run network totals were not recoverable, so recovery counters are not whole-run totals. No whole-Internet or exhaustive search claim is made. Verified collection: 815/815 repository queries attempted; 815 successful, 0 partial, 0 failed and 106 bounded/truncated. All 23 model tasks were attempted; 0 failed. These are bounded discovery attempts, not an exhaustive census or quality approval. Separate from successful search queries, 43 watchlist fetches and 4 enrichment attempts failed; the detailed warnings remain in the report. A 404/409 is an availability gap, not proof of abandonment or license revocation.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

## Coverage

| Audio, music & voice | Fresh OpenSuno primary documentation broadens music discovery. New local voice/music guides separate CPU/GPU profiles, optional model downloads, character/voice rights and hosted services; author audio is not an independent listening test. ## Search and review counts

96 creative fields; 815/815 repository queries attempted; 23/23 model-task queries attempted. 38553 distinct source candidates and 970 model leads. 28 detailed profiles and 161 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 106 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: Fresh GitLab source reads found afsh4ck/openedit and agent-caption with actual code and complete MIT grants. MotionNet had documentation but no implemented code in its root; Xotion’s full software grant is PolyForm Noncommercial and is excluded. AudioStellar and PREMIERE toolbox were also checked. This is a bounded investigation, not all of GitLab. [Source](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit) [Source](https://gitlab.com/ahkamboh/agent-caption) [Source](https://gitlab.com/ahkamboh/xotion-studio) [Source](https://gitlab.com/Roxanne_Ardary/motionnet) [Source](https://gitlab.com/ayrsd/audiostellar/-/raw/audiostellar2/README.md)
- **codeberg** · searched: Direct public HTTP captures successfully returned current Imaginer and Raspberry-Pi-5-Diffusion repository pages, including their documented AI image context. Search-service robots restrictions remained visible and were not bypassed; these two successful primary pages do not establish a complete host-wide search or a newly cleared software grant. [Source](https://codeberg.org/Imaginer/Imaginer) [Source](https://codeberg.org/MightyOwlbear/Raspberry-Pi-5-Diffusion)
- **sourcehut** · searched: Fresh diffuser Cargo.toml and the full MIT license were read through normal public source URLs. Its dependency currently points to an author-local diffusers-rs path, a concrete reproducibility gap. No new ready-to-install SourceHut release or exhaustive host coverage is claimed. [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/Cargo.toml) [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **packages** · searched: Fresh official PyPI JSON for game-localizer/gameloc and versioned creative packages, the TensorStack NuGet index (through 0.14.7), and the official Hugging Face any-to-any task page broadened discovery. Registry labels and model-task support do not clear a project’s complete grant, backend compatibility or quality. [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/game-localizer/json) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/gameloc/json) [Source](https://api.nuget.org/v3-flatcontainer/tensorstack.media/index.json) [Source](https://huggingface.co/tasks/any-to-any)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: Fresh Krita AI Handbook documentation supplements live source review of Lightroom, ComfyUI, Blender, After Effects, Ableton and CAD-agent extensions. Host licensing, model weights and platform-specific acceptance were separated from plugin source grants. No plugin was installed. [Source](https://docs.interstice.cloud/) [Source](https://github.com/nicolasdiolez/mask-bridge) [Source](https://github.com/david2002ochs-droid/blender-codex) [Source](https://github.com/nostoi-labs/livecli)
- **project-sites** · searched: Fresh primary sites covered image/music tools, virtual try-on, furniture design, lighting and other creative practices. Thin JavaScript shells, login placeholders and an OpenSlides TLS failure remain evidence limits; marketing alone admitted no new app. [Source](https://tryonlabs.github.io/opentryon/) [Source](https://shopprentice.com/) [Source](https://lacylights.com/) [Source](https://openslides.dev)
- **research-code** · searched: Fresh author pages/papers covered motion/choreography, neural acoustics, light fields, codecs, calligraphy, inverse yarn modeling and print/scan watermarking. Three PDF downloads were bounded at 2 MB; other raw PDF captures were not full-method/code-license reviews. A paper/demo never substitutes for actual implemented code and a full grant. [Source](https://midotronn.github.io/MAESTRO/) [Source](https://google-research.github.io/seanet/brain2music/) [Source](https://luoprojectpage.github.io/callirewrite/) [Source](https://www.matthewtancik.com/stegastamp)
- **international** · searched: Fresh Chinese, Japanese, Korean-context, Spanish, Persian, Turkish, Urdu/Hinglish and Arabic primary sources broadened screening. Current code/host/model terms were checked without treating language, research origin or popularity as a confidence tier. [Source](https://abdallah28r.github.io/taarib/en/) [Source](https://github.com/jramos681-prog/photo-editorial-skills) [Source](https://github.com/sma6871/persian-translation-polisher) [Source](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit) [Source](https://gitlab.com/ahkamboh/agent-caption)

## openedit (afsh4ck)

Video, animation & film · Editing, captions & post-production · Motion capture & character animation · Accessible media & assistive creation

The repository was created on 2026-10-15. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Original software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. Independent quality and full reproduction remain unverified.

Scope: Prepare reviewed transcript-based cuts, animated overlays, captions and a local multi-track edit from owned footage.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-10. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The entire current GitLab MIT grant, copyright, notice condition and liability disclaimer were read; third-party models, assets and services retain separate terms. [Source](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The author documents a built-in 62-second showcase made from inspectable clips plus UI screenshots. Demo clips and self-checks do not prove complete real-model editing or codec acceptance. [Source](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Install Node 20+, FFmpeg and a recent Chromium browser with WebCodecs; clone the GitLab source, npm install and npm run app. The app opens on loopback port 4747. For AI tasks separately sign in to the chosen Claude Code/Codex client; Whisper setup is optional in the subtitles panel. Independent reproduction was not established. [Source](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** Workflow-specific support and unresolved-blocker review remain incomplete.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or third-party working integration was not established for this workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Node.js 20+, FFmpeg and Chromium/WebCodecs; optional local Whisper, signed-in AI-agent CLI and platform-specific processing features. MIT original editor code. Local Whisper weights, the signed-in agent service, optional speech/dubbing models, codecs and source-media assets have their own terms. A local editor does not mean cloud-agent context remains on-device. [Source](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; not installed or run by AI Media Scout. This is a different project from the existing AH64-dll/OpenEdit record. Mac background-removal features are platform-specific. Published token/4K60/automatic-quality claims are not independently verified; cloud-agent operations can transmit supplied context and do not guarantee a finished edit.

### How it uses AI

A locally hosted browser video editor with LLM-driven editable operation plans, local Whisper captions and agent/MCP integration. [Source](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A locally hosted browser video editor with LLM-driven editable operation plans, local Whisper captions and agent/MCP integration. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Prepare reviewed transcript-based cuts, animated overlays, captions and a local multi-track edit from owned footage. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The author documents a built-in 62-second showcase made from inspectable clips plus UI screenshots. Demo clips and self-checks do not prove complete real-model editing or codec acceptance. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Install Node 20+, FFmpeg and a recent Chromium browser with WebCodecs; clone the GitLab source, npm install and npm run app. The app opens on loopback port 4747. For AI tasks separately sign in to the chosen Claude Code/Codex client; Whisper setup is optional in the subtitles panel. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/blob/main/README.md)

1. Install Node 20+, FFmpeg and a recent Chromium browser with WebCodecs; clone the GitLab source, npm install and npm run app. The app opens on loopback port 4747. For AI tasks separately sign in to the chosen Claude Code/Codex client; Whisper setup is optional in the subtitles panel.
2. For source instructions, open a terminal in the checked-out project folder and choose the documented route for your platform. Release installers follow their own steps.

```sh
git clone https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit.git
```


```sh
cd openedit
```


```sh
npm install
```


```sh
npm run app
```

### First project

Start with a short representative test and inspect the result before batching. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/blob/main/README.md)

1. Open the supplied demo project before using original footage.
2. Import a short owned clip and inspect transcription and editable silence cuts.
3. Ask the configured agent for a bounded edit, then preview, tweak, accept or discard its proposed operations.
4. Export a short test and inspect codec, sound, framing and captions in the actual output.
### Hardware & software

Requirements are documented for the selected route; other numeric minima are not inferred. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** No minimum RAM, VRAM or total disk budget is documented; source media, codecs and selected models determine additional resources.
- **Software:** Node.js 20+, FFmpeg and Chromium/WebCodecs; optional local Whisper, signed-in AI-agent CLI and platform-specific processing features.
- **Platforms:** macOS and Linux prerequisite examples are documented; README also describes Windows Whisper setup. Individual encoder/OS feature acceptance is not universal.

### License, model weights & costs

The complete original software MIT permission, notice and disclaimer were reviewed. Separate components are not relicensed. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT original editor code. Local Whisper weights, the signed-in agent service, optional speech/dubbing models, codecs and source-media assets have their own terms. A local editor does not mean cloud-agent context remains on-device.
- **Commercial:** Original code follows MIT. Model/service/media and font rights are separate and have not all been cleared.
- **Cost:** Local compute and disk plus any separately chosen AI model/service costs; no unsupported promise of zero operating cost.

### Why it merits attention

Included for a concrete AI-media workflow and a reviewed original-code grant. Complete documentation does not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

This is a different project from the existing AH64-dll/OpenEdit record. Mac background-removal features are platform-specific. Published token/4K60/automatic-quality claims are not independently verified; cloud-agent operations can transmit supplied context and do not guarantee a finished edit. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit)
- [Documentation](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/blob/main/README.md)

## agent-caption

Video, animation & film · Audio, music & voice · Accessible media & assistive creation

The repository was created on 2026-10-31. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Original software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. Independent quality and full reproduction remain unverified.

Scope: Prepare editable subtitle drafts and captioned interview or lyric-video copies, including mixed Hindi/English source material.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-10. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The entire current GitLab MIT grant, copyright, notice condition and liability disclaimer were read; third-party models, assets and services retain separate terms. [Source](https://gitlab.com/ahkamboh/agent-caption/-/blob/master/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The author links an 11-language caption demo and caption-health outputs. Claims of perfect or frame-accurate output are not an independent alignment benchmark, and language coverage differs between recognizer and aligner. [Source](https://gitlab.com/ahkamboh/agent-caption/-/blob/master/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use Python 3.10+ and FFmpeg. Obtain this GitLab source, then follow its documented python setup.py environment/model preparation and python caption.py path-to-media entry point. Setup downloads models; the included agent skill is optional and was not executed here. Independent reproduction was not established. [Source](https://gitlab.com/ahkamboh/agent-caption/-/blob/master/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** Workflow-specific support and unresolved-blocker review remain incomplete.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or third-party working integration was not established for this workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Python 3.10+, FFmpeg and separately downloaded Whisper/WhisperX/MMS alignment and optional Demucs components; first-time setup needs network access. Original captioning code is MIT. Recognition/alignment/separation weights and bundled fonts retain their own licenses; the software grant does not establish commercial rights for all MMS/models or input music/voices. [Source](https://gitlab.com/ahkamboh/agent-caption/-/blob/master/README.md)

Documentation review only; not installed or run by AI Media Scout. The README distinguishes 99-language recognition, a smaller WhisperX alignment set and broader MMS alignment—not universally accurate recognition in 1,100 languages. A supplied script fixes words, not every alignment error. GitHub links in the README were unavailable during this review; this profile uses the actual current public GitLab code and full grant.

### How it uses AI

A local Python captioning pipeline combines Whisper transcription, forced alignment, optional music vocal separation and styled subtitle rendering. [Source](https://gitlab.com/ahkamboh/agent-caption/-/blob/master/README.md)

### Introduction

A local Python captioning pipeline combines Whisper transcription, forced alignment, optional music vocal separation and styled subtitle rendering. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/ahkamboh/agent-caption/-/blob/master/README.md)

- **Commercial:** Original code follows MIT. Model/service/media and font rights are separate and have not all been cleared.

## StegaStamp

Images & design · Neural watermarking for printed creative media

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Artist-book, exhibition-photograph and printed-image experiments that connect a physical image to a small machine-readable message.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-10. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/tancik/StegaStamp/blob/master/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The author project page and README show the original, imperceptible residual and photographed print workflow. These are author results, not an independent robustness or security test. [Source](https://github.com/tancik/StegaStamp/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://www.matthewtancik.com/stegastamp)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** The official guide requires Python 3, TensorFlow (tested with 1.13), a recursive source checkout and requirements.txt. The referenced pretrained encoder/decoder and detector must be obtained separately; no current compatibility test was performed. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/tancik/StegaStamp/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://www.matthewtancik.com/stegastamp)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** The complete root code license is MIT. Pretrained encoder, decoder and detector files are linked separately, without separately verified model-weight terms in this review; detector training code is not included. Local computing and optional printer/camera costs; no required paid hosted inference service is documented. [Source](https://github.com/tancik/StegaStamp/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://www.matthewtancik.com/stegastamp)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The default model accepts a UTF-8 secret of at most seven characters after error-correction overhead. Detector outputs can be noisy and stamp-size-sensitive. TensorFlow 1.13 is a historical tested environment, not a verified modern installation. This is not a universal watermark, authenticity or provenance guarantee.

### How it uses AI

A learned encoder/decoder hides a short message in a photograph and recovers it after printing and camera capture; the repository supplies training, image encoding/decoding and detector code. [Source](https://github.com/tancik/StegaStamp/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://www.matthewtancik.com/stegastamp)

### Introduction

A learned encoder/decoder hides a short message in a photograph and recovers it after printing and camera capture; the repository supplies training, image encoding/decoding and detector code. [Source 1](https://github.com/tancik/StegaStamp/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.matthewtancik.com/stegastamp)

### What it is good for

Artist-book, exhibition-photograph and printed-image experiments that connect a physical image to a small machine-readable message. [Source 1](https://github.com/tancik/StegaStamp/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.matthewtancik.com/stegastamp)

### Demo & examples

The author project page and README show the original, imperceptible residual and photographed print workflow. These are author results, not an independent robustness or security test. [Source 1](https://github.com/tancik/StegaStamp/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.matthewtancik.com/stegastamp)

### Install

The official guide requires Python 3, TensorFlow (tested with 1.13), a recursive source checkout and requirements.txt. The referenced pretrained encoder/decoder and detector must be obtained separately; no current compatibility test was performed. [Source 1](https://github.com/tancik/StegaStamp/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.matthewtancik.com/stegastamp)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. For source instructions, open a terminal in the checked-out project folder and choose the documented route for your platform. Release installers follow their own steps.
3. The official guide requires Python 3, TensorFlow (tested with 1.13), a recursive source checkout and requirements.txt. The referenced pretrained encoder/decoder and detector must be obtained separately; no current compatibility test was performed.

```sh
git clone --recurse-submodules https://github.com/tancik/StegaStamp.git
```


```sh
cd StegaStamp
```


```sh
pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
python encode_image.py saved_models/stegastamp_pretrained --image test_im.png --save_dir out/ --secret Hello
```


```sh
python decode_image.py saved_models/stegastamp_pretrained --image out/test_hidden.png
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/tancik/StegaStamp/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.matthewtancik.com/stegastamp)

1. Obtain the documented pretrained encoder/decoder; inspect its terms before use.
2. Encode a short test message into an image, retain the original and residual, then decode the generated image.
3. For a physical experiment, print and photograph a sample and inspect decoding reliability before embedding it in an artwork. Detection of several stamps uses a separate model.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/tancik/StegaStamp/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.matthewtancik.com/stegastamp)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python 3, TensorFlow tested at 1.13, repository submodules and listed Python dependencies. A camera/printer is needed for the physical experiment; no compatible printer model is specified.
- **Platforms:** Supported operating systems are not documented in the reviewed README; a Python interface does not establish equal compatibility on macOS, Windows or Linux.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/tancik/StegaStamp/blob/master/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/tancik/StegaStamp/blob/master/README.md) [Source 3](https://www.matthewtancik.com/stegastamp)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** The complete root code license is MIT. Pretrained encoder, decoder and detector files are linked separately, without separately verified model-weight terms in this review; detector training code is not included.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. The complete root code license is MIT. Pretrained encoder, decoder and detector files are linked separately, without separately verified model-weight terms in this review; detector training code is not included.
- **Cost:** Local computing and optional printer/camera costs; no required paid hosted inference service is documented.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/tancik/StegaStamp/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.matthewtancik.com/stegastamp)

### Limitations

The default model accepts a UTF-8 secret of at most seven characters after error-correction overhead. Detector outputs can be noisy and stamp-size-sensitive. TensorFlow 1.13 is a historical tested environment, not a verified modern installation. This is not a universal watermark, authenticity or provenance guarantee. [Source 1](https://github.com/tancik/StegaStamp/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.matthewtancik.com/stegastamp)

The plugin is free; ready-to-install builds are offered pay-what-you-want from zero on the author page. Lightroom Classic licensing is separate. Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Matte is a binary silhouette estimated from Lightroom renders: gradients, soft brush strokes and opacity are not retained. Outputs are external files, not editable masks reimported into Lightroom. Lower-resolution AI masks can have soft edges; masking does not certify reconstruction or print readiness.

### How it uses AI

Lightroom Classic plugin that uses local RF-DETR segmentation and optional BiRefNet refinement, or exports Lightroom-generated AI masks. [Source](https://github.com/nicolasdiolez/mask-bridge/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/nicolasdiolez/mask-bridge/blob/main/BUILDING.md)

### Introduction

Lightroom Classic plugin that uses local RF-DETR segmentation and optional BiRefNet refinement, or exports Lightroom-generated AI masks. [Source 1](https://github.com/nicolasdiolez/mask-bridge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/nicolasdiolez/mask-bridge/blob/main/BUILDING.md)

Batch-export matched photographs, grayscale masks and optional transparent PNGs for compositing, turntable photogrammetry or Gaussian-splat reconstruction. Get the system archive from the linked author page, unzip and retain MaskBridge.lrplugin, then add it through Lightroom Classic File > Plug-in Manager. Open Library > Plug-in Extras > Mask Bridge Dashboard. A source route is documented in BUILDING.md. [Source 1](https://github.com/nicolasdiolez/mask-bridge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/nicolasdiolez/mask-bridge/blob/main/BUILDING.md)

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/nicolasdiolez/mask-bridge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/nicolasdiolez/mask-bridge/blob/main/BUILDING.md)

1. Select representative photos and choose Generate for COCO-class AI detection or Matte for Lightroom masks.
2. Test edge quality and, where needed, enable slower HD refinement for a small selection.
3. Choose image format, dimensions and matching mask/alpha output.
4. Inspect the exported files before submitting an entire reconstruction batch.

### How it uses AI

A Tauri/React video editor with a built-in MCP server allowing an external AI agent to edit the same visible timeline as the human operator. [Source](https://github.com/Cloudy-Studio-Apps/cloudy-clip/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://tauri.app/start/prerequisites/)

### Introduction

A Tauri/React video editor with a built-in MCP server allowing an external AI agent to edit the same visible timeline as the human operator. [Source 1](https://github.com/Cloudy-Studio-Apps/cloudy-clip/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://tauri.app/start/prerequisites/)

### What it is good for

Agent-assisted short-form rough cuts, multi-track trim/split operations, proxy-assisted editing and undoable timeline changes. [Source 1](https://github.com/Cloudy-Studio-Apps/cloudy-clip/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://tauri.app/start/prerequisites/)

### Demo & examples

The README documents timeline operations and concrete MCP tool names. No independent complete edit/export benchmark was established; AI is supplied by the connected agent, not an embedded generative video model. [Source 1](https://github.com/Cloudy-Studio-Apps/cloudy-clip/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://tauri.app/start/prerequisites/)

### Install

Obtain the source, install Node 20+, pnpm, stable Rust and the platform Tauri prerequisites. Use pnpm install then pnpm tauri dev. Put FFmpeg and ffprobe on PATH for pro-format proxies; Linux H.264 playback additionally needs GStreamer libav. [Source 1](https://github.com/Cloudy-Studio-Apps/cloudy-clip/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://tauri.app/start/prerequisites/)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. For source instructions, open a terminal in the checked-out project folder and choose the documented route for your platform. Release installers follow their own steps.
3. Obtain the source, install Node 20+, pnpm, stable Rust and the platform Tauri prerequisites. Use pnpm install then pnpm tauri dev. Put FFmpeg and ffprobe on PATH for pro-format proxies; Linux H.264 playback additionally needs GStreamer libav.

```sh
pnpm install
```


```sh
pnpm tauri dev
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Cloudy-Studio-Apps/cloudy-clip/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://tauri.app/start/prerequisites/)

1. Import a short local video and inspect playback.
2. Connect the chosen agent to the documented loopback MCP endpoint on port 7420.
3. Ask it to import, place and split a clip; inspect timeline state and preview frames.
4. Undo an agent edit and compare the project before progressing to a longer edit.

- **Software:** Node 20+, pnpm, stable Rust, OS-specific Tauri prerequisites; FFmpeg/ffprobe for pro formats and gst-libav on Linux. The agent client is separate.
- **Platforms:** Windows and Linux release build targets documented; Other Tauri host environments require platform prerequisites; a native Mac release/acceptance was not established

### Introduction

Rust video editor with a headless CLI and MCP server that let an external AI agent modify the same .kerf timeline as the desktop studio. [Source 1](https://github.com/elyesghazel/kerf/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Rough-cut vertical or landscape footage, generate contact sheets for an agent, trim silence, add titles and render locally or on another SSH host. [Source 1](https://github.com/elyesghazel/kerf/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Build the source using cargo build --release for the headless CLI or cargo build --release -p kerf-app for the studio. Install ffmpeg and ffprobe separately. 3. Build the source using cargo build --release for the headless CLI or cargo build --release -p kerf-app for the studio. Install ffmpeg and ffprobe separately. The documented desktop environment needs ALSA, Wayland and X11 client libraries.

```sh
cargo build --release
```


```sh
cargo build --release -p kerf-app
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/elyesghazel/kerf/blob/main/README.md)

1. Create a small project using a documented vertical preset.
2. Import two clips with append, inspect the contact sheet and edit the timeline.
3. Connect a separately chosen AI agent through kerf mcp and inspect its changes in the studio.
4. Export locally first; remote export additionally needs an explicitly configured SSH/rsync host.

- **Creative results · documented:** The README links a saved real local HTTP review and explicit saved-response export, alongside historical model failures. A final complete browser round trip is explicitly still unverified. [Source](https://github.com/misakamikotoghr-5205/caption-relay/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Install Node.js 22.13+ and npm plus local Ollama. Prepare the documented qwen2.5:0.5b model, start Ollama only if not already running, then npm ci and the loopback development server on port 5190. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/misakamikotoghr-5205/caption-relay/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.

- **Models, services and costs · documented:** App code is MIT. Ollama and Qwen weights are external dependencies under their respective licenses, not redistributed by the app. Small-model inference remains fallible. No cloud inference, accounts or API keys are documented for the local workflow; local compute and model download remain necessary. [Source](https://github.com/misakamikotoghr-5205/caption-relay/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. No transcription, translation, overlap repair, rich subtitle styling or accessibility certification. Original timestamps remain unchanged. The model can choose unsuitable or no candidates; nominal timers are not hard wall-clock guarantees. This is not a hardened multiuser service.

### How it uses AI

A local subtitle-review prototype that asks an Ollama model to select or retain literal glossary candidates, with per-cue human decisions and a saved audit trail. [Source](https://github.com/misakamikotoghr-5205/caption-relay/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A local subtitle-review prototype that asks an Ollama model to select or retain literal glossary candidates, with per-cue human decisions and a saved audit trail. [Source 1](https://github.com/misakamikotoghr-5205/caption-relay/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Terminology handoff for plain SRT/VTT while preserving original timestamps and rejecting arbitrary model-written replacements. [Source 1](https://github.com/misakamikotoghr-5205/caption-relay/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README links a saved real local HTTP review and explicit saved-response export, alongside historical model failures. A final complete browser round trip is explicitly still unverified. [Source 1](https://github.com/misakamikotoghr-5205/caption-relay/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Install Node.js 22.13+ and npm plus local Ollama. Prepare the documented qwen2.5:0.5b model, start Ollama only if not already running, then npm ci and the loopback development server on port 5190. [Source 1](https://github.com/misakamikotoghr-5205/caption-relay/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. For source instructions, open a terminal in the checked-out project folder and choose the documented route for your platform. Release installers follow their own steps.
3. Install Node.js 22.13+ and npm plus local Ollama. Prepare the documented qwen2.5:0.5b model, start Ollama only if not already running, then npm ci and the loopback development server on port 5190.

```sh
ollama pull qwen2.5:0.5b
```


```sh
npm ci
```


```sh
npm run dev -- --hostname 127.0.0.1 --port 5190
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/misakamikotoghr-5205/caption-relay/blob/main/README.md)

1. Load the included original SRT or a plain SRT/VTT copy.
2. Enter a literal source=target glossary and run AI review.
3. Inspect tool traces and accept or retain each cue individually.
4. Download subtitle files and audit JSON; repair any timing overlaps in a separate editor.

- **Platforms:** Local browser plus Node/Ollama runtime; specific native OS acceptance is not established

- **Weights:** App code is MIT. Ollama and Qwen weights are external dependencies under their respective licenses, not redistributed by the app. Small-model inference remains fallible.

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/yamasoo/text2comics/blob/main/README.md)

1. First create a copy of the bundled sample project including images and inspect its PDF/CBZ output.
2. For your own chapter, confirm the LLM and ComfyUI environment cards and selected models.
3. Review adaptation plan, script, storyboards and character cards before confirming them.
4. Generate one panel at a time, pick images, correct lettering and export the chapter.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/yamasoo/text2comics/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** The author says model files occupy tens of GB, with additional panel images. No RAM/VRAM minimum is documented. Bundled sample images allow a no-GPU walkthrough, not no-GPU fresh image generation.
- **Software:** Windows 10/11 is the documented tested platform; Python 3.10+, browser, ComfyUI with MOSS-Image unet/clip/vae files; DeepSeek API or an OpenAI-compatible local llama.cpp server.
- **Platforms:** Windows 10/11 tested by the author; other native platforms not established

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/yamasoo/text2comics/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/yamasoo/text2comics/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** Tool code is MIT; MOSS image models, ComfyUI, the chosen text model/service, fekit and other vendor components have separate terms. Their commercial eligibility is not established by the app MIT grant.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. Tool code is MIT; MOSS image models, ComfyUI, the chosen text model/service, fekit and other vendor components have separate terms. Their commercial eligibility is not established by the app MIT grant.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/yamasoo/text2comics/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Configured cloud AI receives the chapter content despite the locally hosted UI. Character appearance, text fidelity and panel selection need human approval. One ComfyUI model set is attached at a time; local LLM VRAM may need releasing before image inference. PDF/CBZ export does not establish print-production readiness. [Source 1](https://github.com/yamasoo/text2comics/blob/main/README.md)

- [Documentation](https://github.com/yamasoo/text2comics/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

A local Web Audio DAW exposes musical project state and bounded editing actions to compatible AI agents through MCP or a CLI, while retaining editable tracks and shared undo.
```sh
git clone https://github.com/smouj/cocoloop.git
```


```sh
cd cocoloop
```

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/smouj/cocoloop/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/smouj/cocoloop/blob/main/docs/AGENT-API.md)

2. Edit a short beat, then optionally enable agent editing, read the real track IDs and submit a small action batch.
3. Audition and undo changes as needed, export WAV/MIDI and save a portable project with its samples.

- **Creative results · documented:** The README provides library screenshots and a wing-flap-reference example request. No independent quality or large-library test was established. [Source](https://github.com/MasoPass/FrameVault/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** For Windows, use the 2.0.0 installer or portable ZIP with bundled Java and FFmpeg. Source builds use JDK 21 and the documented PowerShell scripts. AI integration separately needs Python and the MCP server requirements, then the configured local MCP adapter. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Semantic interpretation comes from the external AI client, not an embedded model. The app listens locally, but an agent may send selected images to its configured provider. Scene-change extraction, comparison and large-library pagination remain roadmap work.

### How it uses AI

A reference library for animation, game development and VFX exposes actual downscaled images, project notes and tagged clip frames to AI agents over MCP, with attributed reversible writes. [Source](https://github.com/MasoPass/FrameVault/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A reference library for animation, game development and VFX exposes actual downscaled images, project notes and tagged clip frames to AI agents over MCP, with attributed reversible writes. [Source 1](https://github.com/MasoPass/FrameVault/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Building an art-direction reference board an agent can inspect, annotate and reuse when discussing poses, timing, style or visual effects. [Source 1](https://github.com/MasoPass/FrameVault/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README provides library screenshots and a wing-flap-reference example request. No independent quality or large-library test was established. [Source 1](https://github.com/MasoPass/FrameVault/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

For Windows, use the 2.0.0 installer or portable ZIP with bundled Java and FFmpeg. Source builds use JDK 21 and the documented PowerShell scripts. AI integration separately needs Python and the MCP server requirements, then the configured local MCP adapter. [Source 1](https://github.com/MasoPass/FrameVault/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. For source instructions, open a terminal in the checked-out project folder and choose the documented route for your platform. Release installers follow their own steps.
3. For Windows, use the 2.0.0 installer or portable ZIP with bundled Java and FFmpeg. Source builds use JDK 21 and the documented PowerShell scripts. AI integration separately needs Python and the MCP server requirements, then the configured local MCP adapter.

```sh
pip install -r mcp-server/requirements.txt
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/MasoPass/FrameVault/blob/main/README.md)

1. Create a project, import an image or a short clip, choose frame count/crop and add tags.
2. Connect a compatible AI client, ask it to inspect a small reference pack and append style/timing notes.
3. Inspect attributed edits and undo unwanted changes. Use the system-tray Quit command when finished.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/MasoPass/FrameVault/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Bundled Windows release contains a Java runtime and FFmpeg/ffprobe. Source build needs JDK 21; MCP adapter needs Python, MCP SDK and Pillow. A Python version minimum is not documented.
- **Platforms:** Windows releases and PowerShell source setup are documented. A macOS build is on the roadmap; Linux/macOS support is not inferred from Java or a web UI.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/MasoPass/FrameVault/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/MasoPass/FrameVault/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** FrameVault code is MIT; bundled FFmpeg is LGPL. Reference images/clips and external AI models/services have separate rights and processing terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. FrameVault code is MIT; bundled FFmpeg is LGPL. Reference images/clips and external AI models/services have separate rights and processing terms.
- **Cost:** Local library plus any chosen AI client/model charges; source-media licensing remains the user’s responsibility.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/MasoPass/FrameVault/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Semantic interpretation comes from the external AI client, not an embedded model. The app listens locally, but an agent may send selected images to its configured provider. Scene-change extraction, comparison and large-library pagination remain roadmap work. [Source 1](https://github.com/MasoPass/FrameVault/blob/main/README.md)

- [Documentation](https://github.com/MasoPass/FrameVault/blob/main/README.md)

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Evidence checked: 2026-11-10. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/POMXARK/voice-studio/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The author reports a 16-line RTX 5080 generation comparison and a large RPG dubbing project. Those author benchmarks do not establish another GPU’s speed, pronunciation accuracy or perceptual quality. [Source](https://github.com/POMXARK/voice-studio/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/POMXARK/voice-studio/blob/main/docs/NOTES.ru.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** The Windows-first setup uses Python 3.10+ with qwen-tts, a project/game configuration copied from config.example.json and python server.py at port 8777. An optional separate fast environment uses the author’s batched fork and version-specific CUDA/PyTorch dependencies; inspect those separately before choosing it. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/POMXARK/voice-studio/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/POMXARK/voice-studio/blob/main/docs/NOTES.ru.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Original code is MIT. The repository includes no game audio, text or model files. Qwen/Whisper/CosyVoice/CAM++ and acceleration-fork grants are separate; voice-actor rights and game EULAs remain relevant. The author frames generated game voices for personal/mod use; commercial voice/game clearance is not established. Local GPU compute and model storage; any game, voice or asset rights are separate. [Source](https://github.com/POMXARK/voice-studio/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/POMXARK/voice-studio/blob/main/docs/NOTES.ru.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. WER, timbre and duration checks are aids rather than substitutes for listening. The advertised sub-second result is a specific author RTX 5080 batch test. Unmerged acceleration patches and game-specific adapters need separate validation.

### How it uses AI

A local dubbing workbench generates cloned dialogue with Qwen3-TTS or other adapters, checks it with Whisper and timbre/length heuristics, then manages retries, takes and game installation. [Source](https://github.com/POMXARK/voice-studio/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/POMXARK/voice-studio/blob/main/docs/NOTES.ru.md)

### Introduction

### Install

The Windows-first setup uses Python 3.10+ with qwen-tts, a project/game configuration copied from config.example.json and python server.py at port 8777. An optional separate fast environment uses the author’s batched fork and version-specific CUDA/PyTorch dependencies; inspect those separately before choosing it. [Source 1](https://github.com/POMXARK/voice-studio/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/POMXARK/voice-studio/blob/main/docs/NOTES.ru.md)

3. The Windows-first setup uses Python 3.10+ with qwen-tts, a project/game configuration copied from config.example.json and python server.py at port 8777. An optional separate fast environment uses the author’s batched fork and version-specific CUDA/PyTorch dependencies; inspect those separately before choosing it.

Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. A local render does not mean agent prompts/contact sheets remain on-device. Author CPU rendering is slow for long projects, manual FilmCraft edits must be communicated to the agent, and the default review server uses plain HTTP for a trusted local network. No independent deployment/security review is claimed.

### How it uses AI

An AI-agent film-editing pipeline combines local Whisper transcripts, contact sheets, beat-aligned edit plans, OpenTimelineIO/FCP7 timelines, FilmCraft rendering and timecoded human review. [Source](https://github.com/aaronblankenburg/cutter/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/aaronblankenburg/cutter/blob/main/requirements.txt)

### Introduction

An AI-agent film-editing pipeline combines local Whisper transcripts, contact sheets, beat-aligned edit plans, OpenTimelineIO/FCP7 timelines, FilmCraft rendering and timecoded human review. [Source 1](https://github.com/aaronblankenburg/cutter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/aaronblankenburg/cutter/blob/main/requirements.txt)

### What it is good for

Iterating a documentary or travel-film edit with an agent while keeping original footage separate and collecting comments on each cut. [Source 1](https://github.com/aaronblankenburg/cutter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/aaronblankenburg/cutter/blob/main/requirements.txt)

### Demo & examples

The author describes seven cuts of one real travel film on Linux and supplies a synthetic demo. This is author use, not independent validation; Apple Silicon and WSL2 routes remain untested on real hardware in this source. [Source 1](https://github.com/aaronblankenburg/cutter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/aaronblankenburg/cutter/blob/main/requirements.txt)

### Install

Install Docker and Git for the documented host, obtain cutter, then use cutter doctor and the agent setup command or CLI. The launcher pulls/builds the container on first use; FilmCraft and Python dependencies are in the image. An AI client and its model access are separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/aaronblankenburg/cutter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/aaronblankenburg/cutter/blob/main/requirements.txt)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. For source instructions, open a terminal in the checked-out project folder and choose the documented route for your platform. Release installers follow their own steps.
3. Install Docker and Git for the documented host, obtain cutter, then use cutter doctor and the agent setup command or CLI. The launcher pulls/builds the container on first use; FilmCraft and Python dependencies are in the image. An AI client and its model access are separate.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/aaronblankenburg/cutter
```


```sh
cd cutter
```


```sh
./bin/cutter doctor
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/aaronblankenburg/cutter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/aaronblankenburg/cutter/blob/main/requirements.txt)

1. Create a new project pointing to a short folder of source footage; give the agent a target length, style and music-rights constraint.
2. Analyze footage, inspect transcript/contact sheets, then have the agent write and review the edit plan.
3. Build/export one cut, inspect it, and collect timecoded feedback before another revision. A fresh clone’s bin/cutter must be invoked directly or placed on PATH before bare cutter commands work.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/aaronblankenburg/cutter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/aaronblankenburg/cutter/blob/main/requirements.txt)

- **Hardware:** No GPU is required for the documented CPU route; Linux NVIDIA acceleration is optional. The image is about 1.8 GB installed/0.7 GB download and Whisper about 1.6 GB, plus project/export storage. RAM and VRAM minima are not published.
- **Software:** Docker, Git and a compatible agent (author tested Claude Code/Codex). The image carries FilmCraft, FFmpeg and pinned Python 3.12 dependencies. Reviewers need a browser.
- **Platforms:** Linux x86_64 was used for one film. Apple Silicon macOS support is documented but not yet hardware-tested by the author; Windows requires WSL2/Docker and is untested.

- **Weights:** Original code is MIT; the container includes FilmCraft MIT/Apache, FFmpeg GPL and separate Python dependencies. Whisper weights, source footage and music have separate terms. Agent provider processing/charges are separate from local transcription.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. Original code is MIT; the container includes FilmCraft MIT/Apache, FFmpeg GPL and separate Python dependencies. Whisper weights, source footage and music have separate terms. Agent provider processing/charges are separate from local transcription.
- **Cost:** Local compute/storage, any chosen AI agent/model service, and properly licensed media/music.

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/aaronblankenburg/cutter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/aaronblankenburg/cutter/blob/main/requirements.txt)

[Source 1](https://github.com/Open-LLM-VTuber/Open-LLM-VTuber/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Open-LLM-VTuber/Open-LLM-VTuber/blob/main/LICENSE-Live2D.md) [Source 3](https://open-llm-vtuber.github.io/docs/quick-start)

### Install

Use a stable release archive with the frontend or clone recursively, then install dependencies with uv sync. Copy the default configuration template to conf.yaml, select LLM/ASR/TTS routes and launch uv run run_server.py. The documented basic setup uses local Ollama/ASR but network Edge TTS, so it is not wholly offline. [Source 1](https://github.com/Open-LLM-VTuber/Open-LLM-VTuber/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Open-LLM-VTuber/Open-LLM-VTuber/blob/main/LICENSE-Live2D.md) [Source 3](https://open-llm-vtuber.github.io/docs/quick-start)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. For source instructions, open a terminal in the checked-out project folder and choose the documented route for your platform. Release installers follow their own steps.
3. Use a stable release archive with the frontend or clone recursively, then install dependencies with uv sync. Copy the default configuration template to conf.yaml, select LLM/ASR/TTS routes and launch uv run run_server.py. The documented basic setup uses local Ollama/ASR but network Edge TTS, so it is not wholly offline.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/Open-LLM-VTuber/Open-LLM-VTuber --recursive
```


```sh
cd Open-LLM-VTuber
```


```sh
uv sync
```


```sh
uv run run_server.py
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Open-LLM-VTuber/Open-LLM-VTuber/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Open-LLM-VTuber/Open-LLM-VTuber/blob/main/LICENSE-Live2D.md) [Source 3](https://open-llm-vtuber.github.io/docs/quick-start)

1. Choose your own authorized avatar and configure its persona and expression mapping.
2. Configure compatible speech/text backends and start the local server.
3. Open the local client in the recommended Chrome browser and test one short voice interaction.
4. Check latency, interruption, pronunciation and data sent to providers before any public performance.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/Open-LLM-VTuber/Open-LLM-VTuber/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Open-LLM-VTuber/Open-LLM-VTuber/blob/main/LICENSE-Live2D.md) [Source 3](https://open-llm-vtuber.github.io/docs/quick-start)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python >=3.10,<3.13 in the deployment guide, Git submodules, uv, FFmpeg and browser/client. Cloud and local backends have different model/runtime requirements; remote microphone access requires a secure context.
- **Platforms:** Windows; macOS; Linux; individual backend/desktop feature support varies

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/Open-LLM-VTuber/Open-LLM-VTuber/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/Open-LLM-VTuber/Open-LLM-VTuber/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Open-LLM-VTuber/Open-LLM-VTuber/blob/main/LICENSE-Live2D.md) [Source 4](https://open-llm-vtuber.github.io/docs/quick-start)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** Core software is MIT with an explicit exception for Live2D sample assets. Those samples have separate character-specific, attribution and commercial conditions; they are not open-source code or freely reusable artwork. Models, SDKs and selected voice/LLM services require their own review.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. Core software is MIT with an explicit exception for Live2D sample assets. Those samples have separate character-specific, attribution and commercial conditions; they are not open-source code or freely reusable artwork. Models, SDKs and selected voice/LLM services require their own review.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/Open-LLM-VTuber/Open-LLM-VTuber/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Open-LLM-VTuber/Open-LLM-VTuber/blob/main/LICENSE-Live2D.md) [Source 3](https://open-llm-vtuber.github.io/docs/quick-start)

### Limitations

No fixed minimum RAM/VRAM/storage is documented for all backends. The project is planning a v2 rewrite while v1 bug fixes continue; that alone is not a quality verdict. Camera/screen input and cloud services can transmit private content; fully offline operation requires appropriate local choices. [Source 1](https://github.com/Open-LLM-VTuber/Open-LLM-VTuber/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Open-LLM-VTuber/Open-LLM-VTuber/blob/main/LICENSE-Live2D.md) [Source 3](https://open-llm-vtuber.github.io/docs/quick-start)

### Get the tool

- [Documentation](https://github.com/Open-LLM-VTuber/Open-LLM-VTuber/blob/main/README.md)

## 3D Guide Generator

3D, reconstruction & assets · Creative learning & authoring · AI parametric design and jewelry

The repository was created on 2026-11-09. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Learn how to construct a reference object while distinguishing observed tutorial evidence from invented hidden geometry and dimensions.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-10. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/RAFAEL-SOSA-UH/3d-guide-generator/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The source includes examples for input modes and a build-once comparison-image path. Author token estimates and model menus are not verified model availability, guide accuracy or geometric acceptance. [Source](https://github.com/RAFAEL-SOSA-UH/3d-guide-generator/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Obtain the source, install Python 3.10+ and requirements.txt plus FFmpeg for video. Run guidegen.py on an included image; choose one supported modeling engine. Direct calls currently use a separately configured Claude provider; other assistants receive an image/prompt package for copy and paste. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/RAFAEL-SOSA-UH/3d-guide-generator/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT covers code, profiles and templates. Third-party CAD hosts, AI providers and input videos/images have separate terms. A guide inferred from a reference is not a design-rights or manufacturing clearance. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/RAFAEL-SOSA-UH/3d-guide-generator/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Direct provider calls are currently Claude-only; model names, token costs and accuracy are not certified. An image can reveal neither hidden geometry nor tolerances, and a successful render does not validate a functional part. No minimum RAM/VRAM/disk budget is documented.

### How it uses AI

Packages video keyframes or reference images into AI modeling guides that label steps as visible, deduced or proposed, with optional separate build checks. [Source](https://github.com/RAFAEL-SOSA-UH/3d-guide-generator/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

Packages video keyframes or reference images into AI modeling guides that label steps as visible, deduced or proposed, with optional separate build checks. [Source 1](https://github.com/RAFAEL-SOSA-UH/3d-guide-generator/blob/main/README.md)

Obtain the source, install Python 3.10+ and requirements.txt plus FFmpeg for video. Run guidegen.py on an included image; choose one supported modeling engine. Direct calls currently use a separately configured Claude provider; other assistants receive an image/prompt package for copy and paste. [Source 1](https://github.com/RAFAEL-SOSA-UH/3d-guide-generator/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.

3. Obtain the source, install Python 3.10+ and requirements.txt plus FFmpeg for video. Run guidegen.py on an included image; choose one supported modeling engine. Direct calls currently use a separately configured Claude provider; other assistants receive an image/prompt package for copy and paste.

```sh
pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh

```

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/RAFAEL-SOSA-UH/3d-guide-generator/blob/main/README.md)

1. Use guide-only mode first and select Blender, FreeCAD, Fusion 360, OpenSCAD or Onshape.
2. Inspect the source/deduced/proposed labels and token estimate before a paid call.
3. Follow the guide manually and check dimensions and hidden surfaces against your actual reference.
4. If separately authorized, build-once may produce a script/render comparison in supported tools; repeated build-and-fix remains planned.

Experimental desktop workflow connecting voice/image AI generation and Claude/OpenSCAD part creation with model preview, slicing and explicitly approved printer jobs. [Source 1](https://github.com/JI-s-BS/morphix/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Explore supervised spoken-idea-to-model prototypes before independently validating any printer or physical part. [Source 1](https://github.com/JI-s-BS/morphix/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README describes a hackathon prototype and its intended Afinia QD330 integration. The award is not evidence that dimensions, slicer output, print control or camera safeguards are validated. [Source 1](https://github.com/JI-s-BS/morphix/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Install Node 22/npm, obtain the source and npm ci, then build the Electron app with npm run build. Create a private config using the provided environment template and launch in windowed mode. Cloud features remain unavailable without separate provider access. [Source 1](https://github.com/JI-s-BS/morphix/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. For source instructions, open a terminal in the checked-out project folder and choose the documented route for your platform. Release installers follow their own steps.
3. Install Node 22/npm, obtain the source and npm ci, then build the Electron app with npm run build. Create a private config using the provided environment template and launch in windowed mode. Cloud features remain unavailable without separate provider access.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/JI-s-BS/morphix.git
```


```sh
cd morphix
```


```sh
npm ci
```


```sh
npm run build
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/JI-s-BS/morphix/blob/main/README.md)

1. Start in windowed preview mode with a disposable model task.
2. Configure only the chosen Gemini/Meshy/Claude route and inspect the generated geometry and dimensions.
3. Review a local slicer preview separately; do not treat a mesh or preview as fabrication approval.
4. The physical-printer route requires its documented hardware validation and explicit user approval; monitoring is experimental.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/JI-s-BS/morphix/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Node 22 and an Electron-supported graphical desktop. Pi scripts target Raspberry Pi 4 Linux/Wayland with systemd user services. Optional PrusaSlicer, OpenSCAD, Python/NumPy/OpenCV and an Afinia QD330-compatible setup are separate.
- **Platforms:** Raspberry Pi graphical Linux is the documented deployment target; Other Electron host systems are not proof of printer integration compatibility

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/JI-s-BS/morphix/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/JI-s-BS/morphix/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT project code. Gemini/Meshy/Anthropic accounts, generated model terms, optional cloud infrastructure and hardware/printer host software are separate and may incur charges.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT project code. Gemini/Meshy/Anthropic accounts, generated model terms, optional cloud infrastructure and hardware/printer host software are separate and may incur charges.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/JI-s-BS/morphix/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Physical printing requires actual machine, material and dimensional checks; Print Guardian does not replace supervision. Other printers are not automatically supported. No RAM/VRAM/storage minimum is documented; service ports and older example addresses require local configuration. [Source 1](https://github.com/JI-s-BS/morphix/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/JI-s-BS/morphix)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/JI-s-BS/morphix/blob/main/README.md)

Prepare WebView2 if needed, open the model manager and choose the five-component profile that fits the GPU. Source UI/service builds use npm and Rust; inference weights are not in installers. [Source 1](https://github.com/timoncool/MiniMax-Music3-Studio/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. For source instructions, open a terminal in the checked-out project folder and choose the documented route for your platform. Release installers follow their own steps.
3. For the main documented path get the Windows x64 installer or portable archive from official releases. Prepare WebView2 if needed, open the model manager and choose the five-component profile that fits the GPU. Source UI/service builds use npm and Rust; inference weights are not in installers.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/timoncool/MiniMax-Music3-Studio/blob/main/README.md)

1. Select a model profile only after checking its full download and VRAM requirements.
2. Enter a short caption and lyrics, generate one track and audition the result.
3. Retain the exact request with the saved audio; compare a variation before requesting stems or MIDI.
4. Download optional capabilities only when needed and check the actual exported audio/MIDI rather than the preview alone.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/timoncool/MiniMax-Music3-Studio/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Documented Music3 profiles: 8 GB card/6.5 GB download (Minimal), 9.5 GB/7.7 GB (Light), 11.5 GB/9.8 GB (Balanced), 15 GB/12.8 GB (Quality), 30 GB/28.6 GB (Full Native). Optional LoRA training needs RTX 30-series+ with at least 22 GB VRAM and about 10.5 GB additional disk. No system-RAM minimum is documented.
- **Software:** Windows 10/11 x64 main path; NVIDIA GTX 900-series or newer is documented, with CUDA 13/driver 580+ for newer cards and CUDA 12 for older architectures or drivers 525–579. WebView2 is needed for the Windows UI. Source builds additionally need npm/Rust and backend toolchains.
- **Platforms:** Windows x64 NVIDIA is the author-tested main path; Linux x86-64 packages and AMD/Intel/CPU alternatives are explicitly experimental; macOS Metal build instructions exist; main-path Windows acceptance does not transfer to Mac

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/timoncool/MiniMax-Music3-Studio/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/timoncool/MiniMax-Music3-Studio/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** Studio original code is MIT according to the complete root grant. The bundled audioMotion-analyzer visualizer component is AGPL-3.0 and must be considered separately in distribution. Music3 weights use a separate community license with attribution/safeguard requirements described by the author, not the software AGPL. Optional models and OpenRouter services need independent review.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. Studio original code is MIT according to the complete root grant. The bundled audioMotion-analyzer visualizer component is AGPL-3.0 and must be considered separately in distribution. Music3 weights use a separate community license with attribution/safeguard requirements described by the author, not the software AGPL. Optional models and OpenRouter services need independent review.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/timoncool/MiniMax-Music3-Studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

GPU architecture support does not mean sufficient VRAM. Optional experimental ports/backends do not make the assessed Windows/NVIDIA workflow Experimental by themselves. No independent musical evaluation, general model-term clearance or benchmark was established. [Source 1](https://github.com/timoncool/MiniMax-Music3-Studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/timoncool/MiniMax-Music3-Studio)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/timoncool/MiniMax-Music3-Studio/blob/main/README.md)
